| Agreement | Adopt the following TP to TS38.213 Clause 8.3 in principle.<br>\| 8.3 PUSCH scheduled by RAR UL grant ... A UE can be provided in BWP-UplinkCommon a set of numbers of repetitions for a PUSCH transmission with PUSCH repetition Type A that is scheduled by a RAR UL grant or by a DCI format 0_0 with CRC scrambled by a TC-RNTI. The UE does not apply sbfd-Config2-Transmission, if provided for a PUSCH transmission with repetitions. If the UE requests repetitions for the PUSCH transmission [11, TS 38.321], the UE transmits the PUSCH over slots, where is indicated by the 2 MSBs of the MCS field in the RAR UL grant or in the DCI format 0_0 from a set of four values provided by numberOfMsg3-RepetitionsList or from {1, 2, 3, 4} if numberOfMsg3-RepetitionsList is not provided. The UE determines an MCS for the PUSCH transmission by the 2 LSBs of the MCS field in the RAR UL grant or by the 3 LSBs of the MCS field in the DCI format 0_0, and determines a redundancy version and RBs for each repetition as described in [6, TS 38.214]. For unpaired spectrum operation, the UE determines the slots as the first slots starting from slot where slots are determined as described in [6, TS 38.214] a repetition of the PUSCH transmission does not include a symbol indicated as downlink by tdd-UL-DL-ConfigurationCommon or indicated as a symbol of an SS/PBCH block with index provided by ssb-PositionsInBurst and where is provided by cellSpecificKoffset; otherwise, if not provided, . For paired spectrum operation, the UE determines the slots as the first slots starting from slot where is provided by cellSpecificKoffset; otherwise, if no provided, . \|<br>Final CR in R1-2610973 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610795](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610795.zip)<br>`[127-R19-AI 8.1]` |

| Agreement | Adopt the following TP for TS 38.214<br>\| Reason for change According to the current specification in Clause 5.2.1.4.2 of TS 38.214, a list of higher layer parameter reportQuantity values that the UE does not expect when configured with CSI sub-configurations (csi-ReportSubConfigToAddModList) is specified. However, 'cli-RSSI' and 'cli-SRS-RSRP' are missing from this exclusion list. Since sub-configurations are defined for CSI derivation based on NZP CSI-RS port subsets, power offsets, and resource lists, they are inapplicable to CLI measurement reporting, which relies on dedicated CLI measurement resource sets. Summary of change Add 'cli-RSSI' and 'cli-SRS-RSRP' to the list of reportQuantity values that the UE does not expect to be configured when csi-ReportSubConfigToAddModList is present in Clause 5.2.1.4.2. Consequences if not approved The specification remains incomplete regarding invalid parameter combinations for CSI sub-configurations, leading to potential ambiguity between gNB configuration and UE reporting expectations for CLI measurement. \|<br>\| 5.2.1.4.2 Report quantity configurations *** Unchanged parts are omitted *** If the UE is configured with a CSI-ReportConfig that contains a list of sub-configurations, provided by csi-ReportSubConfigToAddModList: - The UE expects to be configured with the higher layer parameter codebookType set to 'typeI-SinglePanel', 'typeI-MultiPanel' or 'typeI-SinglePanel-r19' and the higher layer parameter valueOfM is not configured. If the UE indicates a capability for supporting mixed codebook combination in a slot with mixCodeBookSpatialAdaptation, each sub-configuration which is configured with portSubsetIndicator can be configured with the higher layer parameter codebookType set to 'typeI-SinglePanel' or 'typeI-MultiPanel'. - Each sub-configuration can be configured with an antenna port subset using - the higher layer bitmap parameter portSubsetIndicator, of length up to 32 bits, which contains the bit sequence , where is the MSB and is the LSB, bit corresponds to antenna port , and is the number of ports nrofPorts configured for the CSI-RS resources(s) within a NZP-CSI-RS-ResourceSet contained in the CSI-ResourceConfig for channel measurement that corresponds to the CSI-ReportConfig. A bit value 0 in portSubsetIndicator indicates that the corresponding antenna port is disabled for the sub-configuration, whereas bit value 1 indicates that the antenna port is enabled and belongs to the antenna port subset for the sub-configuration. For the derivation of PMI, antenna ports corresponding to all bits with value of 1 in portSubsetIndicator are mapped to consecutive antenna ports starting at CSI-RS antenna port 3000 in increasing order of the bit position in portSubsetIndicator, or - the higher layer bitmap parameter portSubsetIndicator-r19, of length 48, 64 or 128 bits, which contains the bit sequence , where is the MSB and is the LSB, bit corresponds to the port index, after CSI-RS resource aggregation, in order of increasing port index. A bit value 0 in portSubsetIndicator-r19 indicates that the corresponding antenna port is disabled for the sub-configuration, whereas bit value 1 indicates that the antenna port is enabled and belongs to the antenna port subset for the sub-configuration. For the derivation of PMI, port indices corresponding to all bits with value of 1 in portSubsetIndicator-r19 are mapped to consecutive port indices in increasing order of the bit position in portSubsetIndicator-r19. The number of enabled antenna ports can be 48, 64 or 128 for a sub-configuration configured with codebookType set to 'typeI-SinglePanel-r19' or 2, 4, 8, 12, 16, 24 or 32 for a sub-configuration configured with codebookType set to 'typeI-SinglePanel', according to UE capability. - If a sub-configuration is configured with an antenna port subset, then the sub-configuration can be configured with a ri-Restriction, codebookMode and, if the number of antenna ports of the subset greater than 2, with n1-n2 if the higher layer parameter codebookType is set to 'typeI-SinglePanel' or with ng-n1-n2 if the higher layer parameter codebookType is set to 'typeI-MultiPanel', and, if the corresponding number of antenna ports of the subset is 2, with twoTX-CodebookSubsetRestriction, where the parameters ri-Restriction, codebookMode, n1-n2, ng-n1-n2, twoTX-CodebookSubsetRestriction are as described in Clauses 5.2.2.2.1 and 5.2.2.2.2. If a sub-configuration is configured with an antenna port subset, and if higher layer parameter reportQuantity is set to 'cri-RI-i1-CQI', and if the higher layer parameter codebookType is set to 'typeI-SinglePanel', then the sub-configuration is configured with higher layer parameter typeI-SinglePanel-codebookSubsetRestriction-i2, where typeI-SinglePanel-codebookSubsetRestriction-i2 is as described in Clause 5.2.2.2.1. If a sub-configuration is configured with an antenna port subset, and if higher layer parameter codebookType is set to 'typeI-SinglePanel-r19', then the sub-configuration can be configured with codebookMode-r19, typeI-SinglePanelRI-Restriction-r19, typeI-CodebookSubsetRestriction-r19 and typeI-SoftScalingRank-r19, as described in Clause 5.2.2.2.1a. - If a sub-configuration is configured with an antenna port subset, and if the CSI-ReportConfig that contains a mix of sub-configuration(s) each corresponding to 'typeI-SinglePanel' and some other sub-configuration(s) each corresponding to 'typeI-MultiPanel', then the sub-configuration(s) which is configured with portSubsetIndicator is configured with the higher layer parameter codebookMode. - A sub-configuration can be configured with a power offset provided by powerOffset. - A sub-configuration can be configured with a list of NZP CSI-RS resources, provided by nzp-CSI-RS-ResourceList, which indicates one or more NZP CSI-RS resources, within a NZP-CSI-RS-ResourceSet contained in the CSI-ResourceConfig for channel measurement which corresponds to the CSI-ReportConfig. If there is no sub-configuration configured with a power offset provided by powerOffset, the list of NZP CSI-RS resources has no intersection with a list of NZP CSI-RS resources configured for any other sub-configuration(s) within the CSI-ReportConfig, otherwise, the list of NZP CSI-RS resources is identical to or has no intersection with a list of NZP CSI-RS resources configured for any other sub-configuration(s) within the CSI-ReportConfig. A UE does not expect to be configured with nzp-CSI-RS-ResourceList in a sub-configuration if codebookType is set to 'typeI-SinglePanel-r19'. - If a sub-configuration is configured with a list of NZP CSI-RS resources with more than one resource, the UE shall derive the CSI parameters other than CRI conditioned on the reported CRI, where the CRI k (k ≥ 0) for the sub-configuration corresponds to the configured (k+1)-th entry of associated NZP-CSI-RS-Resource in the list of NZP CSI-RS resources. - If a sub-configurations is not configured with nzp-CSI-RS-ResourceList then the sub-configuration shall be associated with all the NZP CSI-RS resources within a NZP-CSI-RS-ResourceSet contained in the CSI-ResourceConfig for channel measurement which corresponds to the CSI-ReportConfig. - the UE reports CSI(s) for one or more sub-configurations according to Clauses 5.2.1.5.1, 5.2.1.5.2, 5.2.3 and 5.2.4, and according to the higher layer parameter reportQuantity configured for that CSI-ReportConfig. - The UE does not expect the higher layer parameter reportQuantity to be set to 'cri-RSRP', 'cri-SINR', 'cri-SINR- Index', 'cri-RSRP-Index', 'none', 'ssb-Index-RSRP', 'ssb-Index-SINR', 'ssb-Index-RSRP- Index', 'ssb-Index-SINR- Index', 'tdcp', 'cjtc-Dd', 'cjtc-F', 'cjtc-Dd-F' or 'cjtc-P', 'p-CRI-r19', 'p-CRI-RSRP-r19', 'p-SSB-Index-r19', 'p-SSB-Index-RSRP-r19', 'rs-PAI-r19', 'csi-PAI-r19 ', 'noneCSI-r19', or 'noneBM-r19', 'cli-RSSI' or 'cli-SRS-RSRP'. \|<br>Final CR in R1-2610992 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610909](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610909.zip)<br>`[127-R19-AI 8.1]` |
| Agreement | Adopt the following TP for TS38.214<br>\| Reason for change According to Clause 6.2.1.3 of TS 38.214, the prioritization and dropping rules for an SRS transmission on one component carrier overlapping in time with a PUCCH or PUSCH transmission on another component carrier are specified by explicitly enumerating individual report quantities. While other CSI report quantities are included, CLI report quantities (L1-CLI-RSSI and L1-SRS-RSRP) are missing from these dropping rules. According to Clause 5.2.5, CSI reports carrying CLI information are associated with k = 1, i.e., the same priority class as CQI/PMI and TDCP reports, which are already included in these dropping rules. Omitting CLI reporting from Clause 6.2.1.3 leaves the UE behaviour undefined when an SRS transmission overlaps with a PUCCH or PUSCH transmission carrying a CSI report that includes CLI information. Summary of change Explicitly add L1-CLI-RSSI to the list of dropped periodic/semi-persistent CSI reports, and L1-CLI-RSSI and L1-SRS-RSRP to the list of dropped aperiodic CSI reports, in Clause 6.2.1.3 of TS 38.214 when overlapping with SRS transmissions on another component carrier. L1-SRS-RSRP is not added to the list for periodic/semi-persistent CSI, since L1-SRS-RSRP reporting can only be aperiodic (Clause 5.2.1.4). Consequences if not approved The specification remains incomplete regarding prioritization and collision handling for CLI reporting against SRS transmissions, leading to potential ambiguity between gNB reception and UE dropping behaviour. \|<br>\| 6.2.1.3 UE sounding procedure between component carriers *** Unchanged parts are omitted *** - the UE shall drop PUCCH/PUSCH transmission carrying periodic/semi-persistent CSI comprising only CQI/PMI/L1-RSRP/L1-SINR/P-L1-RSRP/CSI-PAI/RS-PAI/L1-CLI-RSSI/CLI-RSSI-MRI and/or Type 1 configured grant PUSCH transmission carrying UE initiated CSI report, and/or SRS transmission on a carrier of a serving cell in set configured for PUSCH/PUCCH transmission whenever the transmission and SRS transmission (including any interruption due to uplink or downlink RF retuning time [11, TS 38.133] as defined by higher layer parameters switchingTimeUL and switchingTimeDL of SRS-SwitchingTimeNR) on the carrier of the serving cell happen to overlap in the same symbol - the UE shall drop PUSCH transmission carrying aperiodic CSI comprising only CQI/PMI/L1-RSRP/L1-SINR/TDCP/cjtc-Dd/cjtc-F/cjtc-Dd-F/cjtc-P/P-L1-RSRP/CSI-PAI/RS-PAI/L1-CLI-RSSI/L1-SRS-RSRP/CLI-RSSI-MRI/SRS-RSRP-MRI on a carrier of a serving cell in set whenever the transmission and aperiodic SRS transmission (including any interruption due to uplink or downlink RF retuning time [11, TS 38.133]) as defined by higher layer parameters switchingTimeUL and switchingTimeDL of SRS-SwitchingTimeNR) on the carrier of the serving cell happen to overlap in the same symbol. \|<br>Final CR in R1-2610991 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610909](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610909.zip)<br>`[127-R19-AI 8.1]` |
| Agreement | The FL draft reply LS as below. Final LS in R1-2610954 is endorsed.<br>\| [Draft] Reply LS on UE capability for Mobility Enh Ph5 Title: [Draft] Reply LS on UE capability for Mobility Enh Ph5 Response to: R1-2609014 (R2-2607807) Release: Rel-20 Work Item: NR_Mob_Ph5 Source: RAN1 To: RAN2 Cc: RAN4 1. Overall Description: RAN1 thanks RAN2 for the LS on UE capability for Mobility Enh Ph5. RAN1 discussed the RAN2 agreements on the reuse of the Rel-18/Rel-19 LTM UE capabilities and has no concern. RAN1 confirms that the following UE capabilities, including all their components, can be reused for the LTM SCell activation improvement, where the candidate cell in the field description includes both the LTM candidate SpCell and the LTM candidate SCell: Rel-18: intraFreqL1-MeasConfig-r18, interFreqL1-MeasConfig-r18, ltm-BeamIndicationJointTCI-r18, ltm-MAC-CE-JointTCI-r18, ltm-BeamIndicationSeparateTCI-r18, ltm-MAC-CE-SeparateTCI-r18 Rel-19: intraFreqL1-MeasConfigPeriodicCSI-RS-r19, interFreqL1-MeasConfigPeriodicCSI-RS-r19, intraFreqL1-MeasConfigSP-CSI-RS-r19, interFreqL1-MeasConfigSP-CSI-RS-r19, ltm-BeamIndicationJointTCI-CSI-RS-r19, ltm-MAC-CE-JointTCI-CSI-RS-r19, ltm-BeamIndicationSeparateTCI-CSI-RS-r19, ltm-MAC-CE-SeparateTCI-CSI-RS-r19 RAN1 also confirms that the current value ranges of these capabilities are reused without extension. The PerCell components (maxNumberJointTCI-PerCell-r18, maxNumberDL-TCI-PerCell-r18, maxNumberUL-TCI-PerCell-r18) apply independently to each LTM candidate SpCell and each LTM candidate SCell, while the AcrossCells components and maxNumberCells-r18 apply to the total across LTM candidate SpCells and LTM candidate SCells. RAN1 understands that an LTM candidate SpCell and an LTM candidate SCell are counted separately in maxNumberCells-r18. It is unclear for RAN1 regarding maxNumberCells-r18 and in the AcrossCells TCI-state components when an LTM candidate SCell configured under more than one LTM candidate configurations but would like to leave this for RAN2. 2. Actions: To RAN2: RAN1 kindly asks RAN2 to take the above information into account and to provide the clarification requested above. 3. Date of Next TSG-RAN1 Meetings: TSG RAN WG1 Meeting #128 January 25th – 29th, 2027 Kobe, Japan TSG RAN WG1 Meeting #128bis April 19th – 23rd, 2027 Lyon, France \| | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610919](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610919.zip)<br>`[127-R19-AI 8.1]` |
| Agreement | Adopt the following TP to section 5.1.5 TS 38.214 in principle:<br>\| Reason for change: \| When the unified TCI framework is configured, WUS in RRC_CONNECTED follows the indicated TCI state, the same as other DL channels/signals (RAN1#120bis agreement; TS 38.213 clause 10.4D). Before application of an indicated TCI state, TS 38.214 clause 5.1.5 specifies the QCL assumption of WUS for an LTM cell switch by the LTM Cell Switch Command MAC CE and for a Reconfiguration with sync procedure (based on the SS/PBCH block identified during the random access procedure), and otherwise the SS/PBCH block identified during the initial access procedure. For RACH-less conditional LTM cell switch, the clause specifies the determined CandidateTCI-State only for DM-RS of PDSCH and PDCCH and CSI-RS. After a RACH-less conditional LTM cell switch, there is neither an LTM Cell Switch Command MAC CE nor a random access procedure. WUS therefore falls under the 'otherwise' case and is assumed quasi co-located with the SS/PBCH block of the initial access procedure, which may belong to a different cell, while the other DL channels/signals on the target cell use the determined CandidateTCI-State. TS 38.214 clause 5.1.5 specifies an explicit RACH-based conditional LTM cell switch case for DM-RS of PDSCH and PDCCH and CSI-RS, but not for WUS. \|<br>\|  \|  \|<br>\| Summary of change: \| Add the QCL assumption for WUS for RACH-less conditional LTM cell switch (the reference signal in the determined CandidateTCI-State) and for RACH-based conditional LTM cell switch (the SS/PBCH block identified during the random access procedure), with respect to 'typeC', and when applicable, also with respect to 'typeD', consistent with DM-RS of PDSCH and PDCCH and CSI-RS. \|<br>\|  \|  \|<br>\| Consequences if not approved: \| After a RACH-less conditional LTM cell switch, the UE assumes that WUS is quasi co-located with the SS/PBCH block identified during the initial access procedure, which may belong to a different cell, and may not receive WUS on the target cell; the QCL assumption for WUS after a RACH-based conditional LTM cell switch is not specified consistently with the other DL channels/signals. \|<br>\| ==============Start of changes============== 5.1.5 Antenna ports quasi co-location <Unchanged parts are omitted> After a UE receives an initial higher layer configuration of dl-OrJointTCI-StateList where more than one TCI-State can be used as an indicated TCI state and before application of an indicated TCI state from the configured TCI states: - The UE assumes that DM-RS of PDSCH and DM-RS of PDCCH that are not received during the RACH procedure, WUS, and the CSI-RS applying the indicated TCI state are quasi co-located with the reference signal(s) in the CandidateTCI-State indicated in the LTM Cell Switch Command MAC CE [10, 38.321] if applicable. - The UE assumes that DM-RS of PDSCH and DM-RS of PDCCH and the CSI-RS applying the indicated TCI state are quasi co-located with the reference signal in the determined CandidateTCI-State for RACH-less conditional LTM cell switch [10, 38.321] if applicable. - The UE assumes that DM-RS of PDSCH and DM-RS of PDCCH and the CSI-RS applying the indicated TCI state are quasi co-located with the SS/PBCH block the UE identified during the random access procedure initiated by RACH-based conditional LTM cell switch if applicable. - Otherwise, the UE assumes that DM-RS of PDSCH and DM-RS of PDCCH, WUS, and the CSI-RS applying the indicated TCI state are quasi co-located with the SS/PBCH block the UE identified during the initial access procedure with respect to qcl-Type set to 'typeA', and when applicable, also with respect to qcl-Type set to 'typeD'. ==============End of changes============== \|<br>Final CR in R1-2610986 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610914](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610914.zip)<br>`[127-R19-AI 8.1]` |
| Agreement | Adopt the following TP to section 10.4C TS 38.213:<br>\| Reason for change: \| In 10.4C for LP-WUS, in the formula , the parameter description regarding , is redundant, which should be removed. And there is no reference for the subgroup index in later sentence. \|<br>\|  \|  \|<br>\| Summary of change: \| Remove the parameter for the formula and add the reference for in later sentence \|<br>\|  \|  \|<br>\| Consequences if not approved: \| The formula description and missing reference for is confusing. \|<br>\| 10.4C PDCCH monitoring activation by WUS in RRC_IDLE/RRC_INACTIVE *** Unchanged parts are omitted *** A UE assumes that WUS occasions occur with a periodicity equal to the I-DRX cycle in the RRC_IDLE/RRC_INACTIVE state [17, TS 38.304]. The UE determines WUS occasions associated with a paging occasion based on lpwus-PO-NumPerLO. A first frame of a WUS occasion starts a number of frames prior to the first of a number of paging frames associated with the WUS occasion [17, TS 38.304]. The UE assumes WUS monitoring occasions per periodicity are consecutive in time. A paging occasion associated with a WUS occasion has index where is a number of paging occasions associated with a WUS occasion, , , and are defined in [17, TS 38.304], and is defined in clause 7.1 of [17, TS 38.304]. If a number of subgroups per paging occasion, provided by lp-SubgroupsNumPerPO, is , the codepoint for the subgroup index in a PO is, and the codepoint for all subgroups in the PO is; otherwise, the codepoint for the PO is If, in a WUS monitoring occasion, a UE determines a codepoint associated with the UE [17, TS 38.304], the UE performs PDCCH monitoring according to Type2-PDCCH CSS sets for the paging occasion associated with the WUS monitoring occasion; otherwise, the UE is not required to perform the PDCCH monitoring. The UE may also perform PDCCH monitoring for Type2A-PDCCH CSS sets for DCI format 2_7, if provided. *** Unchanged parts are omitted *** \|<br>Final CR in R1-2610987 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610914](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610914.zip)<br>`[127-R19-AI 8.1]` |
| Agreement | Send reply LS to RAN4 as below. Final LS in R1-2610949 is endorsed.<br>======= Start of Reply LS ========<br>RAN1 is not currently studying channel modelling for non-collocated inter-frequency scenario. The following are answers to questions asked by RAN4.<br>Q1) Whether RAN1 is considering to study channel modelling for non-collocated inter-frequency scenario(s)<br>- Currently RAN1 is not studying channel modelling for non-collocated inter-frequency scenario at this time.<br>Q2) Applicability of the candidate identified by RAN4 which could introduce spatial correlation among non-collocated sites<br>- Option 1: Channel model for ISAC including EO type 2 (TR 38.901 clause 7.9.4 with the Type-2 EO in clause 7.9.5.2) with the urban grid scenario specified in specified in TR 38.914 clause 4.11;<br>  - RAN1 does not recommend Option 1 as the models were intended for environmental models for sensing, and are not intended for enabling inter-BS correlations.<br>- Option 2: The LOS/NLOS state of TRP-UE links modelled according to TR 37.885 clause 6.2 is considered with the urban grid deployment scenario specified in TR 38.914 clause 4.11;<br>  - RAN1 does not recommend Option 2 as LOS/NLOS state correlation is not equivalent to channel spatial/frequency correlation, and may require some changes to TRP-UE links which have not been studied or evaluated.<br>- Option 3: Map-based ray tracing (TR 38.901 clause 8).<br>  - RAN1 does not recommend Option 3, as there are difficulties for alignment (in map, material, and modeling) among companies, deterministic channel generation limiting uses for generalized study, and complexity associated with ray tracing.<br>Q2-1) if applicable, provide guidance if any of the existing evaluation assumptions and deployment scenario can be reused and<br>- None of the options 1, 2, and 3 seem readily applicable from RAN1 perspective to enable the studies outlined by RAN4 and requires further study and discussion in RAN1 to determine potential viable solutions.<br>Q2-2) if yes (to Q2-1), which one can be reused, which parameters or channel-generation procedures require further alignment or clarification for the evaluation of AI-RRM Sub-Case 2, i.e. FR1 L3 non-collocated inter-frequency cell-level prediction.<br>- Option 1, 2, and 3 do not seem readily applicable from RAN1 perspective.<br>RAN1 respectfully asks RAN4 to take above in consideration for discussions on channel modelling for non-collocated inter-frequency scenario.<br>======= End of Reply LS ========<br>Relevant Tdocs: | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610871](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610871.zip)<br>`[127-R19-AI 8.1]` |
| Agreement | Reply to RAN3 LS as below, CC: RAN. Final LS in R1-2611015 is endorsed.<br>---<br>Response to Question 1:<br>- For TRP monostatic sensing, {Delay/range, 3D angle} in C4 and Position in C5 can be mutually transformed, assuming the necessary assistance information, e.g., TRP location, and antenna orientation (LCS to GCS transformation) are available. Doppler and radial velocity can be mutually transformed, assuming carrier frequency is known.<br>- Difference on confidence/uncertainty metrics of C4 and C5 may exist, depending on how they are indicated. For C4, uncertainty is indicated separately for the reported delay and angle metrics, thereby allowing different accuracies in the radial and angular dimensions to be represented. For C5, uncertainty is indicated in position domain through position uncertainty elipsoid. For C5, for example, additional rotation of the uncertainty ellipsoid to the radial direction of the target can be used to approximate the uncertainty result of C4.<br>- The impact of confidence/uncertainty metric of C4 and C5 was not evaluated.<br>Response to Question 2:<br>- RAN1 also considers the same existing specification of UL RTOA and UL AOA for sensing metrics delay and angle of a point as categorized in RAN3 LS. From RAN1 perspective, if C4 is selected by RAN3, certain updates to RAN1 specification for delay and angle measurement definitionsmetrics compared to the two references are expected for sensing. There is no existing definition for Doppler. According to the WID, RAN1 doesn’t have TU to discuss how to define/update them in the normative work of NR ISAC.<br>- Range and radial velocity are respectively equivalent to delay and Doppler for monostatic sensing, if C4 is selected by RAN3.<br>Response to Question 3:<br>- RAN1 also considers the existing specification including the quantization of UL SRS RSRP/RSRPP for the design of sensing power metric of level Ca point. From RAN1 perspective, certain updates inclusion offor power metric in RAN1 specification compared to the existing specification isare expected for sensing. According to the WID, RAN1 doesn't have TU to discuss how to define/update it in the normative work of NR ISAC.<br>Response to Question 4:<br>- RAN1 also considers the existing specification including the quantization of UL SRS RSRP/RSRPP for the design of sensing power metric of level D. From RAN1 perspective, certain updates for inclusion of power metric in RAN1 specification compared to the existing specification isare expected for sensing. RAN1 doesn't have TU to discuss how to define/update it in the normative work of NR ISAC.<br>According to the current WID, RAN1 doesn’t have TU in the normative work of NR ISAC.<br>---<br>Relevant Tdocs: | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610941](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610941.zip)<br>`[127-R19-AI 8.1]` |

| Agreement | Introduce the following Rel. 20 UE FGs (yellow highlighting, if any, shows text that’s not yet agreed)<br>\| 69. NR_MIMO_Ph6 \| 69-5-1 \| Support a periodic, semi-persistent, or aperiodic SRS resource set which includes at least one SRS resource with time-domain resource transmitted across a first S slot and a second consecutive U slot \| 1. One set of time-domain resource allocation related parameters (i.e., startPosition, nrofSymbols, and repetitionFactor) is configured for the SRS resource without restriction on “within a slot”. For the index of each SRS symbol , . The offset counts symbols backwards from the end of the starting slot of the resource ‘’ is the starting symbol in the starting slot for an SRS resource. ‘’ is the symbol index in a slot or two consecutive S and U slots the maximum number of repetition factor is limited to 14 The maximum number of symbols per cross-slot SRS resource is limited to 14 2. Support of PUSCH transmission with a priority index 0 after the cross-slot SRS resource in the U slot 3. Support an aperiodic SRS resource set which includes at least one SRS resource with time-domain resource transmitted in a first S slot, and at least one another SRS resource with time-domain resource transmitted in a second consecutive U slot 4. Support of per-resource slot offset of an aperiodic SRS resource 5. Support of PUSCH transmission with a priority index 0 after all the SRS resources of the set entirely in the U slot 6. Support to allow SRS resource(s) in a non-cross-slot SRS resource set to be transmitted in a serving cell before the same-slot transmission of PUSCH with a priority index 0 and corresponding DMRS in the serving cell. \| FFS \| Yes \| N/A \| a periodic, semi-persistent, or aperiodic SRS resource set which includes at least one SRS resource with time-domain resource transmitted across a first S slot and a second consecutive U slot is not supported \| FFS \| FFS \| FFS \| FFS \|  \| Optional with capability signaling \|<br>\| 69. NR_MIMO_Ph6 \| 69-5-2 \| The available slot(s) can include one slot or two consecutive S and U slots \| The available slot(s) can include one slot or two consecutive S and U slots satisfying there are UL or flexible symbol(s) for the time-domain location(s) for all the SRS resources in the resource set and it satisfies UE capability on the minimum timing requirement between triggering PDCCH and all the SRS resources in the resource set \| FFS \| Yes \| N/A \| The available slot(s) can include one slot or two consecutive S and U slots is not supported \| FFS \| FFS \| FFS \| FFS \|  \| Optional with capability signaling \|<br>\| 69. NR_MIMO_Ph6 \| 69-5-3 \| A single aperiodic SRS resource set configuration for 1T8R via cross-slot \| Support adding a single aperiodic SRS resource set configuration for 1T8R via cross-slot, where eight SRS resources are transmitted in different symbols of two consecutive S and U slots, each mapped to a different UE antenna port. \| FFS \| Yes \| N/A \| 1T8R via cross-slot, is not supported \| FFS \| FFS \| FFS \| FFS \|  \| Optional with capability signaling \| | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-AI 9.6]` |

## 10.2.1 Waveform

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.2 Waveform for 6GR air interface

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | - For uplink DFT-s-OFDM, from RAN1 perspective, support of uplink Frequency Domain Spectrum Shaping (FDSS) with pi/2 BPSK without any spectral extension (SE) or spectral truncation (ST) is recommended.<br>  - The use of FDSS in other context such as FDSS-SE for pi/2 BPSK/QPSK or FDSS-ST for pi/2 BPSK, or FDSS for QPSK is under further RAN1 discussion. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2609140](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2609140.zip) |
| Agreement | Draft LS in R1-2610982 is endorsed in principle with removing “[with pi/2 BPSK without any spectral extension (SE) or spectral truncation (ST)]”. Final LS in R1-2611013 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610982](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610982.zip) |

## 10.3.1 Channel coding

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.3 Channel coding and modulation for 6GR interface

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For Polar code for DCI with payload size larger than NR range (i.e., larger than 140bits),<br>- The maximum mother code length   is 512<br>  - Option 1-1: , a single segment (i.e., no segmentation)<br>  - Option 1-2: , up to 2 segments<br>- The following options for coding scheme are considered:<br>  - Option D1: Remove interleaver<br>  - Option D2: Reuse/Extend D-CRC interleaver<br>Note: The necessity of DCI payload size larger than NR range needs to be confirmed by other agenda(s) | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| Conclusion | There is no consensus to introduce enhancements for encoding of 3-11 UCI bits as compared to 5G RM code. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610588](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610588.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| Agreement | For 3-11 UCI bits, 5G RM code is supported. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610588](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610588.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| Agreement | Consider the following evaluation assumptions for BG3 base graph determination<br>\| Channel \| AWGN \|<br>\| Modulation and code rate \| (6, 719/1024), (6, 772/1024), (6, 822/1024), (6, 873/1024), (6, 910/1024), (6, 948/1024) (8, 682.5/1024), (8, 711/1024), (8, 754/1024), (8, 797/1024), (8, 841/1024), (8, 885/1024), (8, 916.5/1024), (8, 948/1024) (10, 805.5/1024), (10, 853/1024), (10, 900.5/1024), (10, 948/1024) \|<br>\| Code block size (bits with CRC) \| [8448:-128: 3840] (Optional) Company can report the evaluation results for [3840-128: -128:2176] for BG3 base graph determination. \|<br>\| Interleaver \| Same as 5G NR \|<br>\| CRC attachment \| Same as 5G NR \|<br>\| Target CB BLER \| BLER=10-2, 10-4 \|<br>\| Decoding algorithm of LDPC \| Layered BP or normalized min-sum Max number of iteration times: 5,7,10, 15 Decoding order: reversed order Other decoding order, if used, can be reported. Company to report the decoding algorithm, use the same decoding algorithm for the evaluation and comparison of all candidate BGs. \|<br>\| Metrics \| SNR performance difference at the same maximum complexity Maximum complexity performance difference at the same SNR performance \|<br>\| Demodulation algorithm \| Max-log-map \|<br>\| Note 1: Other combination of modulation orders and code rates, with code rate larger than~2/3, can be further evaluated if introduced. Note 2: Company to report the details of decoding algorithm, including LLR clipping threshold, decoding precision for LBP decoding, scaling factor for layer-normalized min-sum decoding algorithm, early termination for LDPC decoder. Note 3: Company to report the detailed interpolation method used in metric evaluation. Note 4: BG3 is not intended to fully overlap with BG1 or overlap with BG2 in terms of TBS and code rate Note 5: The evaluation assumptions for the selection criteria of BG3 when BG1/BG2/BG3 is used will be discussed separately Note 6: The design of new BG considers performance and computational complexity benefits for all applicable modulation order (e.g. 256 QAM and above) and code rates no less than approximately 2/3 at iterations (5, 7,10, 15) compared to BG1 Note 7: The BG3 base graph decision criteria will be separately discussed. Note 8: Use 5G lifting sizes and filler bits for evaluation purpose only Whether/how to enhance the lifting sizes and the location of filler bits can be further discussed. Note 9: If there is a significant imbalance in the reported decoding algorithm, discuss further how to use the simulation results. Note 10: Companies are encouraged to align the performance results based on layered BP decoding algorithm \| Note 1: Other combination of modulation orders and code rates, with code rate larger than~2/3, can be further evaluated if introduced. ## 10.3.2 Modulation

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.3 Channel coding and modulation for 6GR interface

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For performance study to allow a single spectrum efficiency point (subject to quantization error) to be supported by multiple  pairs, to evaluate the performance with 6GR DL codeword to layer mapping, companies are encouraged to provide link level evaluation with link adaptation<br>- The evaluation methodology follows agreement in RAN1-126bis<br>- For rank>1, use 2 codewords for DL and report for UL.<br>- Target 10% first transmission BLER for each codeword. No retransmission in this evaluation<br>- Report rank statistics when rank adaptation is on<br>- The MCS table(s) supporting one single spectrum efficiency point to be supported by multiple  pairs shall use the set of SE points of current NR (DL 1024QAM table and UL 256QAM table respectively for DL and UL AMC evaluation) as starting point of the evaluation.<br>- DL and UL maximum modulation order is 1024QAM<br>For DL link adaptation evaluation, when reporting results, please use a table form<br>- 1st column: SNR (Please use integer SNR points)<br>- 2nd column: Achieved spectrum efficiency with NR DL 1024QAM MCS table<br>- 3rd column: Achieved spectrum efficiency with a single spectrum efficiency point to be supported by multiple  pairs<br>For UL, Companies are encouraged to report link level evaluation with link adaption. When reporting results, please use a table form<br>- 1st column: SNR (Please use integer SNR points)<br>- 2nd column: Achieved spectrum efficiency with NR UL 256QAM MCS table without power backoff adjustment<br>- 3rd column: Achieved spectrum efficiency with a single spectrum efficiency point to be supported by multiple  pairs without power backoff adjustment<br>- 4th column: Achieved spectrum efficiency with NR UL 256QAM MCS table with power backoff adjustment<br>- 5th column: Achieved spectrum efficiency with a single spectrum efficiency point to be supported by multiple  pairs with power backoff adjustment | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610850](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610850.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |

## 10.4.1 Operation in idle mode

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.4 Multiple carrier operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For multiple carriers where, for initial access, a UE can utilize resources for UL (Msg1, etc.) on a carrier other than the carrier on which the UE obtains the system information providing the configuration for those resources, support at least S1 with D1.<br>  - S1: 1 DL physical carrier + N UL physical carriers, where N > 1<br>  - D1: co-located carriers for which a common timing/frequency synchronization relationship can be assumed<br>    - Note: common timing/frequency synchronization relationship are assumed from UE perspective<br>  - Applicable at least to UC-1 (UL coverage enhancement / DL-UL coverage imbalance)<br>  - FFS: Applicability to other use case(s)<br>  - FFS the maximum number of UL physical carriers N<br>  - At least one of the N UL carriers is in the same band as the DL carrier | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in idle mode]` |
| Agreement | For further study of initial access for idle mode multi-carrier operation, consider the following additional potential use case:<br>  - UC-4: Direct access to a wider-bandwidth carrier<br>    - In this use case, an idle/inactive UE camps on a lower-frequency coverage carrier (e.g., FDD) for SI and paging. When [a service requires wider bandwidth and] the UE is within coverage of a wider-bandwidth capacity carrier (e.g., TDD), the UE can use the configuration obtained on the camping carrier to perform initial access directly on the wider-bandwidth carrier.<br>    - Study UC-4 under S3b<br>    - Compare direct initial access on the wider-bandwidth carrier with the following reference procedures.<br>      - Single-carrier initial access on the camping carrier followed by handover to the capacity carrier<br>      - Single-carrier initial access on the camping carrier followed by addition/activation of the capacity carrier as an SCell<br>      - Cell reselection to the wider-bandwidth carrier followed by single-carrier initial access on that carrier<br>      - Cell camping on the wider-bandwidth carrier followed by single-carrier initial access on that carrier<br>    - Compare direct initial access on the capacity carrier with each reference procedure in terms of the following aspects:<br>      - UE synchronization and measurement effort, including at least the time required and the associated UE and NW energy consumption, and failure cases<br>        - Cooperation with RAN2 and RAN4 | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in idle mode]` |
| Agreement | For S1 with D1 assuming UC-1, consider the following aspects for initial access:<br>  - UL access carrier selection<br>    - Take measurement-based selection as the starting point. The UE selects the UL carrier for Msg1 based on measurements on the SI-providing DL carrier.<br>      - FFS details including measurement during WI<br>      - FFS in case the maximum number of UL physical carriers N>2<br>    - FFS: Other candidate options<br>  - Reference signal-to-RO mapping<br>  - UL Tx power control<br>    - Use SSBs on the SI-providing carrier for pathloss estimation.<br>    - FFS how to account for the pathloss difference between that DL carrier and a candidate UL carrier during WI<br>    - Note: other reference signal can be considered if additional reference signal to SSB is supported | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in idle mode]` |
| Agreement | For study of S3a, consider the following aspects for initial access, and decide whether to support S3a and the applicable deployment condition(s) in RAN1#128<br>  - Applicable at least to UC-1 (UL coverage enhancement / DL-UL coverage imbalance)<br>  - FFS: Applicability to other use case(s)<br>  - Consider D2 and D3 for study<br>  - UL access carrier selection<br>    - For UC-1, take measurement-based selection as the starting point. Study the necessity and conditions of UL access carrier selection based on measurements on the non-SI-providing carrier<br>  - Reference signal-to-RO mapping<br>    - Study the necessity and conditions of RO mapping from the SSBs on the non-SI-providing carrier<br>  - UL Tx power control<br>    - Study the necessity and conditions of pathloss estimation based on SSBs on the non-SI-providing carrier<br>  - Synchronization acquisition, UL transmission timing<br>    - Study the necessity and conditions of synchronization acquisition and/or UL transmission timing based on SSBs on the non-SI-providing carrier<br>  - Note: other reference signal can be considered if additional reference signal to SSB is supported | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in idle mode]` |
| Conclusion | For the purpose of RAN1 discussion, for S1 and S3 (S3a and S3b), each of N UL physical carriers can be from a FDD band, a TDD band or an SUL band as defined in 5G NR.<br>- Note: band definition and evaluation of RF on UE is up to RAN4. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in idle mode]` |

## 10.4.2 Operation in connected mode

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.4 Multiple carrier operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For 6GR multiple carrier operation in connected mode, for mechanisms for resource determination of a CORESET:<br>- At least support a CORESET confined within a physical carrier as baseline<br>- Whether to support a CORESET over multiple physical carriers is further discussed<br>  - FFS details for applicable scenario and condition, e.g., where Method 1 is supported, a carrier too narrow for the required aggregation level, etc<br>  - FFS Coordination with other WG(s), e.g., RAN4 (as needed) | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in connected mode]` |
| Agreement | For bandwidth operation with single carrier operation in CONNECTED mode for a UE in 6G,<br>- For DL, conclude the following for PDSCH and PDCCH reception<br>  - The schedulable frequency resources for the PDSCH is either identical to or contained within active “6GR-BW”.<br>  - PDCCH monitoring is within an active 6GR-BW.<br>- Note: Handling of random access in CONNECTED mode to be revisited after random access in IDLE mode has progressed further under the initial access agenda item | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in connected mode]` |
| Agreement | For bandwidth operation with single carrier operation in CONNECTED mode for a UE in 6G,<br>- For DL, conclude the following for PDSCH and PDCCH reception<br>  - The schedulable frequency resources for the PDSCH is either identical to or contained within active “6GR-BW”.<br>  - PDCCH monitoring is within an active 6GR-BW.<br>- Note: Handling of random access in CONNECTED mode to be revisited after random access in IDLE mode has progressed further under the initial access agenda item | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in connected mode]` |
| Agreement | For mechanisms for PXSCH resource allocation in 6GR multiple carrier operation in connected mode, conclude the following:<br>- Support a PXSCH confined within a physical carrier | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in connected mode]` |
| Agreement | For mechanisms for PXSCH resource allocation in 6GR multiple carrier operation in connected mode, conclude<br>- that Rel-20 SI prioritizes the study of single PDSCH over non-contiguous multiple carriers<br>- FFS: single PDSCH over contiguous multiple carriers | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in connected mode]` |
| Agreement | For fast enabling/disabling access to additional carriers for 6GR multiple carrier operation in connected mode, further discuss how to achieve fast enabling access to an additional carrier, considering the following:<br>- For the study, RAN1 assumes the enabling operation is composed of one or more of the following:<br>  - Enabling command processing<br>  - RF preparation, e.g., retuning or switching on an RF module<br>  - Synchronization acquisition, TA and AGC<br>  - CSI acquisition and reporting<br>  - Others<br>- Which of the above steps can be prepared or skipped, shortened before/after the command for enabling, depending on at least one or more of the following:<br>  - UE behaviors in an already-enabled carrier and another to-be-enabled carrier<br>  - The synchronization relationship between a to-be-enabled carrier and another already-enabled carrier, e.g., D1/D2/D3<br>  - Whether the to-be-enabled carrier shares an RF chain with the already-enabled carrier in UE side<br>  - The information kept by the UE for the to-be-enabled carrier in the disabled state, e.g., configuration, measurement, synchronization, CSI<br>- From RAN1’s study, for synchronization acquisition/AGC with synchronization source on the to-be-enabled carrier, at least consider the following candidates:<br>  - SSB<br>  - OD-SSB<br>  - SS-only<br>  - Periodic TRS<br>  - Aperiodic TRS<br>  - Others | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in connected mode]` |

RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| Agreement | For the extended coverage of 153 dB MaxCL in TR 38.914, study how to meet the requirement with relaxed performance requirements compared to basic coverage for one or more channels/signals during initial access, e.g.:<br>- Additional latency, e.g., combining over a larger number of transmissions/periods<br>- Relaxed one-shot detection/decoding target, e.g., a relaxed MDR/BLER for a single transmission of a given signal/channel<br>- Note: the above does not imply there will be RAN4 requirement<br>- Note: The above does not imply that a new basic physical-signal or channel design is required to meet the 153 dB MaxCL target. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| Agreement | Study FDMed paging, where the paging occasions/messages are multiplexed in frequency domain, to facilitate network energy savings | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| Working Assumption | - For 6GR, support<br>  - SSB repetition within one default SSB periodicity: UE is assumed to support SSB combining across different SSB repetitions within one default SSB periodicity by the maximum number of repetitions M and across different default SSB periodicities by the number of N<br>    - 80 ms default SSB periodicity for initial cell search<br>    - Maximum number of repetitions and number of default SSB periodicities for UE to perform SSB combining: M = 2, N = 2<br>      - Fixed number of repetitions as starting point, where M within one SSB period: 2<br>  - 2 symbols for PSS in one 6GR SSB，<br>    - Using 5G NR SSS&PBCH time domain pattern<br>  - A UE in connected mode expects that<br>    - an additional periodic synchronization signal and the SSB transmissions for initial cell search provide an effective periodicity of up to 40 ms for synchronization, RLM and RRM of its serving “Pcell”<br>    - an additional periodic reference signal for finer time and frequency tracking with 20ms periodicity<br>      - FFS: Other periodicity for exceptions<br>  - Strive to specify solutions to reduce the initial cell selection complexity/latency<br>Note: the default SSB periodicity value above is applied at least for TN. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.5 Multi-antenna system › 10.5.1 Initial access and mobility

| Conclusion | - There is no consensus on need for support of more than 64 preambles per RO for increasing preamble capacity. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| Agreement | - Use NR ZC PRACH sequence design, including the applicable unrestricted/restricted sets and sequence lengths, as the baseline 6GR sequence.<br>- Identify the deployment conditions in which sequences do not meet requirements and study how to address, e.g., enhanced single-sequence constructions, and dual-/multi-sequence constructions, with ZC based and non-ZC based sequences. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| Agreement | For 2-step RACH study,<br>- Physical-layer design of 2-step RACH uses NR 2-step RACH (MsgA PRACH, MsgA PUSCH and MsgB) as the starting point.<br>  - Correspondence between MsgA PRACH (preamble/RO) and MsgA PUSCH/DMRS, including the mapping ratio.<br>For RACH-less/contention-based PUSCH (CB-PUSCH) access study, further study the following:<br>- Target UEs and scenarios, e.g., stationary/low-mobility UEs (e.g., FWA, mIoT   such as meters and sensors), and high RSRP UEs, and NTN UEs with GNSS-based timing/frequency pre-compensation.<br>For dedicated RACH (1-step) using a UE-dedicated PRACH preamble study, further study the following:<br>- Applicable RRC states and use cases (e.g., paging-triggered RA, grant-free/shared data transmission, scheduling request for UEs without valid TA).<br>- Assumptions for the number of users in the network and the number of sequences needed for UE dedicated PRACH preamble and justification of feasibility.<br>- Detection performance to detect correct UE dedicated PRACH preamble<br>On the justification of the additional procedure(s) study,<br>- UE RRC states and benefits (e.g., latency, signaling overhead, UE/network energy) relative to 4-step RACH, considering the resource/configuration overhead and UE/BS complexity of supporting 4-step RA and other procedures in a cell. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |

| Agreement | For single-carrier operation during initial access, for the maximum bandwidth of the CORESET for SIB1-PDCCH monitoring:<br>- Down-select the following options for TDM case of SSB and the CORESET for SIB1-PDCCH monitoring in FR1 (including around 7GHz)<br>  - Option A: the maximum is 96 RBs for 15 kHz SCS and 48 RBs for 30 kHz SCS.<br>  - Option B: the maximum is 96 RBs for both SCSs.<br>  - Option C: the maximum is larger than 96 RBs for 15 kHz SCS and larger than 48 RBs for 30 kHz SCS.<br>  - FFS: the maximum for FDM case between SSB and the CORESET for SIB1-PDCCH monitoring<br>- FFS: the maximum for FR2-1<br>- Note: Other candidate values smaller than the above maximum can be discussed in work item. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |
| Agreement | CRB0 indication for the DL carrier on which the SSB for initial cell selection is detected, for single-carrier operation<br>- The UE determines CRB0 from a non-negative RB-level offset counted downward from the lowest-numbered CRB overlapping with SSB.<br>- The offset is in RBs of the CRB-grid SCS; its range and granularity are work-item details. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |
| Agreement | For single-carrier operation during initial access, for the study of Option 2, the maximum bandwidth of schedulable frequency resources for SIB1-PDSCH is down-selected between the following options in FR1 (including around 7GHz)<br>- Option A: the maximum is 96 RBs for 15 kHz SCS and 48 RBs for 30 kHz SCS<br>- Option B: the maximum is 96 RBs for both SCSs<br>- Option C: the maximum is larger than 96 RBs for 15 kHz SCS<br>- Option D: the maximum is larger than 96 RBs for 30 kHz SCS<br>- Option E: the maximum is larger than 48 RBs for 15 kHz SCS<br>- Option F: the maximum is larger than 48 RBs for 30 kHz SCS<br>- Option G: the maximum is larger than 96 RBs for 15 kHz SCS and larger than 48 RBs for 30 kHz SCS.<br>FFS: the maximum is different for the case SSB is outside schedulable frequency resources of SIB1-PDSCH<br>FFS: the maximum for FR2-1<br>Note: Other candidate values smaller than the above maximum can be discussed in work item. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |
| Agreement | For the single carrier operation and for paging PDCCH/PDSCH,<br>- The frequency location and bandwidth of the CORESET for paging-PDCCH monitoring are within the schedulable frequency resources for one paging PDSCH. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |
| Agreement | For the single carrier operation during initial access,<br>- From UE operation perspective, down-select between the following alternatives:<br>  - Alt1: All UL transmissions, including hops if frequency hop is enabled, during RACH procedure are within schedulable frequency resources of Msg3 PUSCH configured by SIB1<br>  - Alt1a: All UL transmissions, including hops if frequency hop is enabled, during one attempt of RACH procedure are within schedulable frequency resources of Msg3 PUSCH configured by SIB1<br>  - Alt2: UL transmissions during RACH procedures may have different frequency location and/or bandwidth for the following:<br>    - ROs for PRACH transmission<br>    - Schedulable frequency resources for Msg3 PUSCH transmissions, including hops if frequency hop is enabled<br>    - Frequency location(s) for PUCCH transmissions for Msg4 HARQ, including hops if frequency hop is enabled<br>- From network configuration perspective:<br>  - FFS: Whether/how multiple sets of configurations can be provided in SIB1<br>  - The frequency location and bandwidth or schedulable frequency resources for UL transmission may be configured independently from schedulable frequency resources for DL reception | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |
| Agreement | For the single carrier operation and for OSI PDCCH/PDSCH,<br>- The frequency location and bandwidth of the CORESET for OSI PDCCH monitoring are within the schedulable frequency resources for OSI PDSCH. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |
| Agreement | For single-carrier operation during initial access, for the multiplexing pattern between the SSB and the CORESET for SIB1-PDCCH monitoring:<br>- TDM between the SSB and the CORESET for SIB1-PDCCH monitoring is supported in all frequency ranges.<br>- Down-select the frequency range(s) in which FDM between the SSB and the CORESET for SIB1-PDCCH monitoring is supported:<br>  - Alt A: FR2 only.<br>  - Alt B: Around 7 GHz and FR2.<br>  - Alt C: All frequency ranges, where the carrier bandwidth allows.<br>  - Other Alternatives<br>- FFS: Whether/how to support Joint time and frequency multiplexing (TDM+FDM) of the SSB and the CORESET for SIB1-PDCCH monitoring. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |

| Conclusion | For MRSS, semi-static assignment of time-frequency resources of 5G NR and 6GR PDCCH transmission, i.e., TDM and/or FDM of non-overlapping NR and 6GR CORESETs, is feasible from the NW perspective in a way transparent to the UE. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610256](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610256.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | On 6GR PDCCH REG-bundle size and precoding granularity, consider the following alternatives. Down select one alternative by RAN1#128:<br>- Alt.1: Do not introduce neither REG bundles sizes larger than 6 REGs nor precoding granularity larger than a REG bundle<br>- Alt.2: Only for REG bundle size of 6 REGs support the same precoder for multiple contiguous REG-bundles in time/frequency<br>  - Alt.2a: Support precoding granularity Option 3 or Option 4 (decide which one) with the number of continuous REG bundles with the same precoding granularity of either 2 or 4 (decide which one).<br>  - Alt.2b: Support precoding granularity Option 2 (wideband precoding)<br>- Alt.3: Introduce REG bundle size of either 12 or 24 REGs (decide which one) and do not support precoding granularity other than a single REG bundle.<br>- FFS, whether channel estimation can be reused and how to count overlapping CCEs of different candidates in Alt.2a and Alt.3.<br>- FFS, how to support interleaved and/or non-interleaved CORESET in Alt.2a and Alt.3.<br>- Note: If there is no consensus to support an alternative, Alt 1 would be supported. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610257](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610257.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - At least time-first frequency-second REG indexing is supported for 6GR CORESET for any CORESET duration<br>  - REGs are numbered in increasing order in a time-first manner, starting with 0 for the first OFDM symbol and the lowest-numbered resource block in the CORESET.<br>  - FFS, how to apply time-first frequency-second REG indexing if CORESET coexistence Option 3 is supported.<br>- FFS, whether and how to support frequency first time-second REG indexing | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610258](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610258.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | For the study of CSI acquisition for PDCCH, consider the following options:<br>- Option 1: NW implementation-based link adaptation, as in NR<br>- Option 2: PDCCH-specific CSI report by the UE<br>  - FFS: What RS/IMs are used to obtain CSI measurements for PDCCH.<br>  - FFS, the content of the PDCCH-specific CSI report or reported preferred configuration (e.g., AL).<br>Option 3: UE reporting of the PDCCH reception outcome, e.g., ACK/NACK for the DCI, or an indication of a missed DCI in the HARQ-ACK feedback.<br>- FFS, evaluation metric(s) and assumptions for comparison of Options 2-3 against Option 1. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610258](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610258.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR PDCCH coverage and reliability, study whether none, one, or multiple techniques need to be supported in addition to ALs up to 16 to meet the target metric of each scenario below, considering:<br>- At least the following techniques:<br>  - AL 32<br>    - Larger ALs up to companies to report<br>  - Intra-slot PDCCH repetitions<br>  - Inter-slot PDCCH repetitions<br>- Assumptions on PDCCH precoding and/or beam gain in link budget computation up to companies to report<br>  - Link budget template from the RAN1 study of "Re-use of existing 5G mid-band (~3.5GHz) site grid for 6G deployments in at least around 7 GHz and targeting comparable coverage to 5G mid-band" is used as a baseline.<br>- At least coverage of CORESET#0<br>  - Evaluation of CORESETs other than CORESET#0, e.g., a UE-dedicated RRC-configured CORESET is not precluded<br>- PDCCH evaluation assumptions in TR 38.830, Clause A.1 and Table A.1-7: “Channel-specific parameters for PDCCH for FR1” are used as a baseline.<br>  - Other values can be reported by companies including the CORESET duration.<br>- At least the following scenarios:<br>  - Deployment around 7 GHz on the 5G mid-band (3.5 GHz) site grid<br>    - Companies to report whether/how PDCCH coverage gap identified in AI 10.5.1.1 is covered<br>  - Lower carriers (e.g., 700 MHz FDD or 2.6 GHz TDD) with 1Rx and 2Rx with 20 MHz CBW<br>    - Companies to report whether/how PDCCH coverage gap identified in AI 10.5.1.1 is covered<br>  - FFS, NTN, considering aiming at a harmonized TN/NTN design and based on the progress in the corresponding AI, including the target metric<br>  - FFS, Lower carriers (e.g., 700 MHz FDD or 2.6 GHz TDD) and CBW less than 20 MHz, including the target metric | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610259](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610259.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |

| Agreement | - Study when there is a UE memory issue considering at least the following factors for L = 2 for single-slot based PDSCH<br>  - Distance between consecutive DMRS symbols and position of the first DMRS symbol<br>  - Maximum number of DL layers<br>  - DMRS density in the frequency domain<br>  - post-FFT buffer related factors<br>    - Maximum number of RBs for PDSCH in the configured bandwidth<br>    - Scheduling offset between PDCCH and PDSCH<br>  - Channel estimation buffer related factors<br>    - FDRA for PDSCH<br>    - TDRA for PDSCH<br>    - Number of scheduled DMRS ports<br>    - Number of co-scheduled DMRS ports<br>    - Number of configured DMRS ports<br>    - [Presence of PT-RS]<br>  - Note: For study purpose, the UE buffer requirement can be compared with that of NR for a reference configuration, e.g. 100 MHz with 4 DL layers<br>- Study the solution when the issue happens, e.g., introduce a scheduling restriction, dynamic indication of L, etc., and the corresponding impact on performance.<br>- Study should consider the impact on network scheduling flexibility on MU-MIMO operation. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610251](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610251.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| Agreement | - For single PDSCH with frequency domain resource allocation up to 400 MHz:<br>  - Support Option A: TB can span up to 400MHz<br>    - Note: Spatial first, frequency second, time third mapping<br>    - Note: FDM of TBs within one PDSCH is not supported<br>  - For PDSCH with two DMRS occasions (L=2) and for allocated BW larger than approximately 200 MHz, further study the applicable time-domain separation between the two DMRS occasions<br>    - Consider impact on UE memory, processing time, performance, and SU/MU-MIMO scheduling<br>- Support frequency interleaving of PDSCH within a single carrier and within:<br>  - For the case of number of scheduled/allocated RBs is equal to or smaller than the number of RBs that approximately corresponds to 200 MHz:<br>    - Frequency interleaving is performed within all scheduled/allocated RBs<br>  - For the case of number of scheduled/allocated RBs is larger than the number of RBs that approximately corresponds to 200 MHz:<br>    - The PDSCH frequency-domain allocation is split into two (semi-)equal parts and frequency interleaving is performed independently within each part<br>  - Scheduled/allocated RBs in the above applies to both RBG-based and RIV-based mapping<br>  - For FR2-1, frequency interleaving, if supported, is discussed separately<br>- Support single FDRA for PDSCH on a single physical carrier with up to 400 MHz CBW, both for the case of one RF chain and two RF chains at the UE<br>  - No restrictions for 400 MHz CBW in case of two RF chains at the UE | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610996](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610996.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |

| Agreement | For 6GR Category A PUCCH:<br>- Support sequence-based transmission for UCI payloads within 1-X bits,<br>  - Down-select X value by RAN1#128, for a given PUCCH Format, from among [value range of X],<br>    - Alt-1. Replace [value range of X] as X = 11<br>    - Alt-2. Replace [value range of X] as X = 6<br>    - Alt-3. Replace [value range of X] as X = 2<br>    - Alt-4. Replace [value range of X] as X <= 11<br>    - Alt-5. Replace [value range of X] as X <= 6<br>    - Alt.6: Replace [value range of X] as 4<=X <= 6<br>    - Alt.7: Replace [value range of X] as 4<=X <= 8<br>  - X is defined as a maximum UCI payload for the PUCCH format using category A defined in the spec.<br>  - FFS: Whether a smaller value than X can be configured as a maximum UCI payload size for a Category A PUCCH Format for a UE.<br>  - FFS: Whether to have multiple X values for multiple PUCCH formats in category A, if multiple formats are supported.<br>- Strive for no more than 2 category A PUCCH formats.<br>- Support a PUCCH resource of 1 RB.<br>- Support a network-configurable number of OFDM symbols for a PUCCH resource within a slot<br>  - The number of OFDM symbols is up to 14<br>- Support multi-UE multiplexing on the same time-frequency resource.<br>- Support at least one of the following candidate formats, with potential down-selection:<br>  - Format A1: Sequence-based transmission with DMRS and with modulation<br>  - Format A2: Sequence-based transmission without DMRS without modulation<br>  - Format A3: Sequence-based transmission with and without DMRS/modulation (e.g., none, some bits or all bits are provided by sequence selection and remaining bits, if any, by modulation based on configuration).<br>  - Format A4: Sequence-based transmission without DMRS with modulation<br>- Evaluate the candidate format(s) (including decision of the value of X) considering at least coverage including PAPR impact, detection performance including DTX detection, base station complexity, and practical multi-UE multiplexing capability ensuring performance requirement, UE complexity & memory.<br>  - Companies to evaluate, as optional, robustness to HARQ-ACK payload size ambiguity<br>    - Evaluate NACK-to-ACK & ACK-to-NACK error and DTX-to-ACK error, when the transmitter uses a payload size 1 bit less than the receiver is expecting<br>  - Use NR PUCCH format as baseline for comparison, when applicable<br>  - Companies to evaluate practical multi-UE multiplexing capability<br>    - Evaluate at least NACK-to-ACK & ACK-to-NACK error and DTX-to-ACK error when multiple UEs are multiplexed<br>    - Evaluate overhead of semi-statically allocated UL channels when multiple UEs are multiplexed<br>FFS:<br>- Candidate numbers of OFDM symbols.<br>- Sequence design details, and number of sequences.<br>- [Whether and how MRSS scenarios are considered for Category A PUCCH.] | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2609611](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2609611.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR Category B PUCCH, support:<br>- DMRS- and channel-coding-based transmission for UCI payloads larger than Y bits<br>  - Down-select Y value by RAN1#128, for a PUCCH Format, from among Y >= 2<br>  - Y is defined, such that Y+1 is a minimum UCI payload for the PUCCH format using category B, and is defined in the spec.<br>  - FFS: Whether to have multiple Y values for multiple PUCCH formats in category B, if multiple formats are supported.<br>- Strive for no more than 2 category B PUCCH formats.<br>- QPSK and pi/2 BPSK modulation.<br>- DFTS-OFDM waveform.<br>  - FFS: Whether CP-OFDM is used for PUCCH with short duration.<br>- A network-configurable number of RBs for a PUCCH resource:<br>  - The number of RBs is less than or equal to [16].<br>  - FFS: The specific supported number of RBs.<br>  - Strive to decide the maximum number of RBs until RAN1#128<br>- A network-configurable duration for a PUCCH resource within a slot.<br>  - The number of OFDM symbols is up to 14<br>  - FFS: The specific supported PUCCH resource durations.<br>FFS:<br>- DMRS design (e.g., pattern, sequence, mapping)<br>  - DMRS and UCI multiplexing details.<br>  - Whether and how to maximize commonality with 6GR PUSCH DMRS, where applicable<br>- Whether/how to support multi-user multiplexing in a same time-frequency resource. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2609611](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2609611.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | Support PUCCH repetition scheme in 6GR<br>- Considering 5G inter-slot PUCCH repetition scheme(s) as a starting point. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2609611](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2609611.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |

| Agreement | For 6GR CSI-RS CDM size and type, prioritize the following candidates for further down-selection per port number basis<br>- Candidate 2-1-1: cdm1<br>- Candidate 2-1-2: cdm2-FD2<br>- Candidate 2-1-3: cdm4-FD2-TD2<br>- Candidate 2-1-4: cdm8-FD2-TD4<br>- Candidate 2-2-1: cdm16-FD4-TD4<br>- Note: cell edge performance should be considered and compared with full-power CSI-RS transmission | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |

| Agreement | To assess whether specification enhancement is needed for SD-precoded CSI-RS,<br>- Consider the following setup:<br>  - The total number of transmitted SD-precoded CSI-RS ports is P, which equals the total number of TXRUs<br>  - UE measures  CSI-RS ports, subsequently calculates and reports the CSI based on the measured  ports, and if applicable, the reconstruction information in Option 2.<br>- Consider the following options for potential down-selection:<br>  - Option 1: Type I-like (W1W2) and/or eType II-like (W1W2Wf) CSI reporting with port-selection (PS) basis with dimension<br>  - Option 2: Type I-like (W1W2) and/or eType II-like (W1W2Wf) CSI reporting with NR DFT basis with dimension   with reconstruction information<br>    - Companies to report how to get the reconstruction information<br>  - Option 3: Type I-like (W1W2) and/or eType II-like(W1W2Wf) CSI reporting with NR DFT basis with  dimension  , including the use of CRI<br>- Note: Companies to provide assumptions on how the precoder for CSI-RS is obtained at the aNB<br>- Benchmark: Type I and eType II codebook with non-precoded CSI-RS with P CSI-RS ports (benchmark 1) or P’ CSI-RS ports (benchmark 2).<br>- The assessment is based on the performance of the options and the benchmark and the UE complexity.<br>- For evaluation, companies are to choose from the following (P, P’) values: (256, 32), (256, 64), (256, 256), (128, 128), (128, 32) | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |

| Agreement | Regarding DL beam measurement and report procedure, for the study on L1-SINR based DL beam measurement and report, the following is considered:<br>- L1-SINR calculation<br>  - NR L1-SINR definition and quantization can be used as the starting point<br>  - FFS: Whether any other extension of L1-SINR definition is needed<br>- Resource requirements for channel and interference measurement<br>  - FFS: Whether/how to introduce interference measurement resource (IMR), besides for channel measurement resource (CMR)<br>- Report content<br>  - L1-SINR per reported CMR, or per reported CMR and its associated IMR(s), if supported.<br>- At least following aspects to be considered for designing L1-SINR based DL beam measurement and report:<br>  - NW flexibility for multiple interference hypotheses to support MU-MIMO pairing (Case-1) or cross-TRP/cell transmission (Case-2), via interference-aware beam selection.<br>  - RS/measurement overhead<br>  - Joint UL/DL CSI acquisition | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |
