| Agreement | RAN1 at least support the following DCIs for 6GR, with the following functions:<br>- (A1) UE-specific DCI, including:<br>  - (B1) For CSS for UE-specific DCI,<br>    - (C1) The fallback DCI for DL and fallback DCI for UL that are for scheduling DL/UL transmission<br>      - (WA) The scheduled UL/DL is based on single-layer transmission,<br>      - Applicable for RRC connected state, and FFS applicability for other RRC states.<br>      - The DCI content does not depend on UE-specific RRC configuration;<br>      - FFS: The fallback DCI for DL and the fallback DCI for UL have the same DCI container size<br>    - (C2) Non-fallback DCI is not supported in CSS for UE-specific DCI.<br>  - (B2) For USS for UE-specific DCI,<br>    - (C3) FFS: UE-specific fallback DCI in USS<br>    - (C4) The non-fallback DCI for DL and non-fallback DCI for UL that are for scheduling DL/UL transmission<br>      - The scheduled UL/DL is based on single or multi-layer transmission<br>      - The DCI content can depend on UE-specific RRC configuration;<br>    - FFS: Non-scheduling/pure control information indication<br>      - For non-scheduling/pure control information indication by UE-specific DCI, strive for avoiding repurposing scheduling DCI fields, the details are left to work item.<br>  - Note: a scheduling DCI can contain non-scheduling/control information<br>- (A2) Cell-specific DCI and DCI for random access procedure, including:<br>  - (B3) For CSS for Cell-specific DCI and DCI for random access procedure<br>    - (C5) DCI(s) with the following functions<br>      - Scheduling on common signals/channels, e.g.,<br>        - Scheduling of system information;<br>        - Scheduling of paging messages, FFS others.<br>      - Scheduling on random access procedure (e.g., scheduling of Msg2/Msg4 in DL, scheduling of Msg3 retransmission in UL);<br>      - FFS: Non-scheduling/pure control information indication<br>      - FFS: whether (C5) DCIs have same size or not<br>  - (B4) No support of USS for Cell-specific DCI<br>- (A3) FFS: Group-specific DCI, including:<br>  - Group-common information or group of UEs’ information indication.<br>- FFS: The CSS for UE-specific fallback DCI can be the same or different from the CSS for cell-specific DCI and DCI for random access procedure.<br>- FFS: The container size of the Cell-specific DCI and DCI for random access procedure in CSS is the same or different as that of the UE-specific fallback DCI in CSS. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2609867](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2609867.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |

| Agreement | Capture the following in the TR section 5.6.1:<br>For RRC connected state, power consumption of PDCCH monitoring is a significant contributor of total UE modem power consumption. Time-domain adaptation of PDCCH monitoring is considered as one of the most effective UE energy saving features for PDCCH. For 6GR, it is recommended to specify time-domain adaptation mechanism for PDCCH monitoring for RRC connected state aiming for simplified and/or unified framework with minimum duplication.<br>Note (not for TR): Detailed mechanism(s) will be discussed in the normative phase based on the agreements achieved during the SI. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2609867](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2609867.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| Agreement | For the study on dynamic adaptation of the number of Rx antennas for PDCCH monitoring, potential specification impact includes:<br>- Adaptation mechanism, signaling and the corresponding UE behavior, including the alignment of the assumed Rx antennas for PDCCH monitoring between network and UE<br>- Relationship between Rx antenna adaptation and PDCCH candidate monitoring<br>- UE switching delay, interruption time and behavior<br>- Radio link monitoring and recovery procedures<br>- PDCCH and PDSCH link adaptation<br>- Note: some of these aspects may have potential impact on other WGs (e.g. RAN2, RAN4) | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2609867](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2609867.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| Agreement | For the DCI monitoring size budget at least for single-carrier operation, RAN1 to agree the following:<br>- The maximum number of monitored container sizes per slot is limited to Xmax (= 4 as the starting point)<br>- As starting point, the UE does not expect to be configured for PDCCH monitoring of more than the Xmax DCI container sizes within a slot.<br>- A separate across-slot budget Zmax (> Xmax)<br>- Xmax and Zmax are fixed in specification.<br>- FFS: for multi-carrier operation, whether the budget is per cell.<br>- FFS: For two-stage DCI | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2609868](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2609868.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| Working Assumption | For the DCI container size determination, RAN1 to agree the following:<br>- For UE-specific non-fallback DCI in RRC connected state, the DCI container size(s) are explicitly configured by the network via RRC (Alt 2);<br>  - FFS: whether Alt 1 is additionally supported as a default rule when no size is configured;<br>  - FFS: for each DCI container, how RRC configures applicable [functionalities/DCIs/DCI contents/DCI formats]<br>- For cell-specific DCI, DCI for random access procedure and fallback DCI (in CSS), the container size(s) do not depend on UE-specific RRC configuration (Alt 3');<br>- FFS: two-stage DCI | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2609868](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2609868.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| Working Assumption | For the study of two-stage DCI, it is assumed that the 1st stage is monitored with a single container size across slots by a UE with blind decoding, and the 2nd stage does not have blind decoding. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2609868](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2609868.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |

| Agreement | - Support UL skipping mechanism for DG-PUSCH and CG-PUSCH in 6GR, while the following aspects are subject to further discussion during the WI phase:<br>  - Detailed skipping rules for cases involving UCI multiplexing.<br>  - FFS: Others, including conditions for skipping the PUSCH and/or signaling for skipping at least for DG-PUSCH<br>- Further study till RAN1#128,<br>  - Whether/how to solve the ambiguity at the base station side when UL-skipping is enabled and no PUSCH is received on the PUSCH resource,<br>    - FFS: the cases involved in the ambiguity<br>  - FFS: Partial skipping using a subset of PUSCH resources | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-DL/UL transmission without dynamic scheduling]` |

## 10.5.4.3 HARQ related Aspects

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.5 Multi-antenna system › 10.5.4 Downlink control channel, scheduling and HARQ operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | In 6GR, consider whether the scheme / conclusion for the DL (when agreed) may be applied to the UL for HARQ retransmission granularity smaller than TB level.<br>- The discussion on the UL will happen after the DL scheme is sufficiently progressed/concluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| Agreement | In 6GR, one UE processing capability is defined for the minimum UE processing timelines agreed in RAN1#126bis, as the baseline UE processing capability.<br>  - The baseline UE processing capability is determined per SCS<br>  - FFS impact of DMRS configuration / pattern, PDSCH length, PDCCH overlapping with PDSCH on the baseline UE processing capability, TB size, PDSCH resource allocation<br>    - FFS: whether/how to avoid multiple processing timelines based on above aspects<br>  - FFS whether multiple UE processing modes within the baseline UE processing capability, is further defined for at least one or more of the following purposes (target to be concluded in RAN1#128)<br>    - UE energy efficient operation (a relaxed processing timeline)<br>    - UE receiver processing based on advanced receiver such as AI/ML receiver (a relaxed processing timeline)<br>  - FFS whether a second UE processing capability should be defined including the motivation and benefits | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610771](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610771.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| Agreement | For downlink HARQ operation in 6GR,<br>- Support a dynamic HARQ-ACK codebook type, based on NR Type-2 as the starting point.<br>- Support one or two additional codebook type(s) based on the characteristics of NR Type-1 codebook and/or the NR (e)Type-3 codebook as the starting point.<br>- For Method 1 (if supported),<br>  - Triggering DCI provides timing and resource indication for HARQ-ACK feedback, and is different from DL DCI(s) with/without scheduling PDSCH for which the corresponding HARQ-ACK feedback is included in the HARQ-ACK codebook.<br>  - DL DCI(s) with/without scheduling PDSCH associated with HARQ-ACK feedback can be used to indicate HARQ-ACK feedback timing and resource.<br>  - The operation with triggering DCI is supported for at least one of the additional codebook type(s).<br>    - FFS whether the triggering DCI is supported for the dynamic HARQ-ACK codebook type<br>  - The operation without triggering DCI is supported for dynamic HARQ-ACK codebook type and at least for one of the additional codebook type(s).<br>- For Method 3 (if supported), the operation with semi-statically configured HARQ-ACK feedback resource is supported for all codebook type(s).<br>- Note: the enhancements of each codebook type are discussed separately, if any. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610772](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610772.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| Agreement | In 6GR, study (till RAN1#128, except last bullet which will be decided in channel coding AI) necessity and whether(/how) one or more of the following UE memory efficient operations including memory size and memory access bandwidth can be supported:<br>- Limiting total number of HARQ processes across carriers/cells<br>- HARQ disabling including HARQ operation disabling, HARQ soft-combining disabling with/without HARQ-ACK feedback disabling<br>- UE report or indication or capability reporting related to the soft-buffer and memory access bandwidth<br>  - How to abstract UE implementation aspects for the UE report<br>- NW indicates priority of HARQ process ID and/or carrier ID for HARQ soft-combining including any indication from UE and UE behaviour corresponding to the NW indication<br>  - This does not imply HARQ retransmission across carriers/cells<br>- LBRM aspects for 6GR<br>  - Note: channel coding AI will discuss and decide whether to support LBRM and channel coding related aspects.<br>  - Note: Impact to LBRM design from channel coding perspective should be avoided from discussion in this AI. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610772](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610772.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |

## 10.5.4.4 Handling of potential multiple uplink transmissions

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.5 Multi-antenna system › 10.5.4 Downlink control channel, scheduling and HARQ operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | In 6GR, for single TRP case, the UE is not expected to be scheduled to transmit a PUSCH that is scheduled with a DCI that overlaps in time with another PUSCH that is scheduled with another DCI in the same carrier.<br>- FFS: for M-TRP case | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Handling of potential multiple uplink transmissions]` |
| Agreement | In 6GR, for single TRP case, a PUSCH that is scheduled with a DCI (DG-PUSCH) overrides an time-overlapping CG-PUSCH on the same carrier, provided that a timeline requirement is satisfied.<br>- FFS: for M-TRP case<br>- FFS: details on the timeline | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Handling of potential multiple uplink transmissions]` |
| Working Assumption | - From collision handling perspective, for a CG-PUSCH configuration that is not immediately activated, the first occasion of the CG-PUSCH that is activated by a DCI is considered as DG-PUSCH. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610703](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610703.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Handling of potential multiple uplink transmissions]` |
| Agreement | For the study of defining a deterministic timeline for processing collision-handling, the timeline for processing collision-handling is independent of the UL channels/signals/UCI scheduled/configured within the time-unit<br>- Note: “separate CSI measurement/computation triggering/configuration and CSI reporting triggering/configuration” was agreed in RAN1<br>- The collision-handling timeline doesn’t consider the CSI measurement and computation process<br>- The collision-handling timeline needs to consider the timeline for CSI report triggering and transmission<br>- Note: details for the timeline for collision-handling at least depend on the details for “separate CSI measurement/computation triggering/configuration and CSI reporting triggering/configuration” in the CSI agenda | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610704](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610704.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Handling of potential multiple uplink transmissions]` |
| Agreement | For the study of one-shot collision-handling (multiplexing/prioritization) for PUCCHs/UCIs based on a time-unit, consider following options.<br>- Option 1: collision-handling is performed for all PUCCHs/UCIs without repetition within the time-unit, irrespective of symbol-level overlap among the associated transmissions.<br>- Option 2: collision-handling is performed for all PUCCHs/UCIs without repetition within the time-unit in case that there is overlapping between at least two PUCCHs. Otherwise, all the PUCCHs transmitted in a TDM manner.<br>- Option 3: collision-handling is performed for all PUCCHs/UCIs without repetition within the time-unit in case that there is overlapping between at least two PUCCHs or there are more than 3 PUCCHs in the time-unit. Otherwise, all the PUCCHs/UCIs transmitted in a TDM manner.<br>- Option 4: collision-handling is performed for all semi-static PUCCHs/UCIs without repetition within the time-unit, and dynamic PUCCH/UCI if it overlaps with any of the resources for semi-static PUCCH(s)/UCI(s).<br>- Option 6: One common list of PUCCH resources for all the UCIs in a time-unit.<br>  - A PUCCH resource is selected based on all the UCIs (after multiplexing/prioritization) in the time-unit<br>  - There is no dedicated PUCCH resource allocation or configuration per UCI type<br>FFS: network signaling based exclusion | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610704](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610704.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Handling of potential multiple uplink transmissions]` |
| Agreement | For network-signalling methods to simplify or mitigate ambiguities in collision-handling procedures for UL channels, signals, and/or UCI, study (till RAN1#128) the scenarios, purposes, and necessity where network signalling may be beneficial, including, for example:<br>- To control the channel(s) for UCI multiplexing in the case of multiple channels.<br>- To control the UCI to be multiplexed in the PUSCH/PUCCH<br>- To exclude an UL channel from collision handling procedure within a time-unit  for TDMed UL transmissions, (if TDMed channels within a time-unit is supported)<br>- Scenarios with ambiguity in determining the target UL transmission for UCI multiplexing.<br>- Consider single-carrier and multi-carrier operation<br>To control whether to multiplex or drop the UCIR1-2610702	FL summary#1 on handling of potential multiple uplink transmissions	Moderator (MediaTek) | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610705](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610705.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Handling of potential multiple uplink transmissions]` |

| Agreement | For SR in 6GR, support 1-bit SR for 6GR. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| Agreement | For UE-to-UE CLI measurement and reporting in SBFD:<br>- Support UE to measure UE-to-UE CLI-RSSI in DL subband.<br>- Support UE to measure CLI-RSSI in UL subband in the time duration of DL reception.<br>- FFS whether to support subband CLI-RSSI measurement and reporting. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| Agreement | Support UE-initiated UE-to-UE CLI (UEI CLI) reporting to the network in 6GR.<br>- This does not require RAN1 to specify RS/channels dedicated for CLI event detection. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| Agreement | For handling BS-to-BS CLI, support non-transparent PUSCH resource muting mechanism for CP OFDM and DFT-s-OFDM.<br>- The design of Rel-19 PUSCH resource muting mechanism is the starting point for design. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| Agreement | The information exchange between BSs for handling BS-to-BS CLI at least supports the following information:<br>- The time domain configuration in TDD and time/frequency configuration of SBFD.<br>- The configuration of BS-to-BS CLI measurement RS: CSI-RS and 6GR SSB.<br>- Non-recommended beam(s), i.e., the strongest DL beam(s) from the victim BS.<br>- BS-to-BS CLI mitigation request from the victim BS.<br>- Note: no normative behaviour is implied for BS receiving the non-recommended beam(s) or the BS-to-BS CLI mitigation request.<br>- FFS other information. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| Agreement | For the sequence design of RIM RS in 6GR, reuse the 5G RIM RS sequence design. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| Agreement | For the frequency domain allocation of RIM RS in 6GR:<br>- For 30KHz subcarrier spacing, min{48, bandwidth of DL carrier in number of PRBs} is supported.<br>- FFS on 96 PRBs for 30KHz subcarrier spacing.<br>- FFS on 15KHz subcarrier spacing | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |

## 10.6.1.1 Design of WUS with OFDM based sequence

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.6 WUS and operation › 10.6.1 Downlink WUS and operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Conclusion | No DL WUS-specific sync signal is introduced in 6GR. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Design of WUS]` |
| Agreement | DL WUS will at least support 12 and 20 PRB bandwidths.<br>FFS: 6 PRBs | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Design of WUS]` |
| Working Assumption | - The maximum number of transmittable sequence candidates per DL WUS sequence resource per cell is 128.<br>  - The maximum number of total information bits is 7. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Design of WUS]` |
| Agreement | - Adopt Zadoff-Chu sequences for 6GR DL WUS as a baseline.<br>- Identify the deployment conditions in which Zadoff-Chu sequences sequences do not meet requirements and study solutions thereof. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Design of WUS]` |
| Agreement | Provide simulation results for at least the following parameter combinations for above 2 agreed basic scenario:<br>\| Version \| Carrier freq. \| # Rx \| #target sequence \|<br>\| 1 \| 700 MHz \| 1 \| 2 \|<br>\| 2 \| 700 MHz \| 1 \| 8 \|<br>\| 3 \| 700 MHz \| 2 \| 8 \|<br>\| 4 \| 3.5 GHz \| 1 \| 2 \|<br>\| 5 \| 3.5 GHz \| 1 \| 8 \|<br>\| 6 \| 3.5 GHz \| 2 \| 2 \|<br>\| 7 \| 3.5 GHz \| 2 \| 8 \|<br>\| 8 \| 7 GHz \| 2 \| 2 \|<br>\| 9 \| 7 GHz \| 2 \| 8 \|<br>Companies are encouraged to use the following configurations as a baseline:<br>\| Parameter \| Value \|<br>\| MDR & FDR/FAR \| 1%, 1% \|<br>\| Max # transmittable candidate sequences per sequence resource \| 128 \|<br>\| Total # info bits \| 7 \|<br>\| Sequence type \| Zadoff-Chu \|<br>\| Seqeunce length \| 139/144 \|<br>\| TD extension method \| Repetitions \|<br>For coverage enhancement evaluations, frequency extension from 12 to 20 PRB replaces a frequency doubling.<br>Other configurations are not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Design of WUS]` |
| Conclusion | Sub-symbol sequence mapping (M = 2) is not supported as an overhead reduction technique for DL WUS in Rel-21 6GR.<br>6 PRB are not supported as an overhead reduction technique for DL WUS in Rel-21 6GR. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Design of WUS]` |

## 10.6.1.2 WUS operation in RRC states

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.6 WUS and operation › 10.6.1 Downlink WUS and operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For evaluating the estimated SINR difference between SINR measured in RRC connected by EE processing and SINR measured in RRC connected by regular processing in RAN1, companies are encouraged to provide results in RAN1#128 with the associated assumptions as listed in the table below.<br>\|  \| Assumed SINR/SNR \| The number of measurement samples \| measurement interval between two adjacent measurement samples \| # of Rx \| Sliding window size(us) \| Other setting by EE processing if any \| The number of measurement samples \| measurement interval between two adjacent measurement samples \| # of Rx \| Sliding window size(us) \| Other setting by non-EE processing if any \| Delta SINR 1 by EE processing, e.g., max (5% delta SINR, 95% delta SINR) \| Delta SINR 2 by regular processing, e.g., max (5% delta SINR, 95% delta SINR) \| Delta SINR 3 e.g., max (5% delta SINR, 95% delta SINR) \|<br>\| Companyname-01 \|  \|  \|  \|  \|  \|  \|  \|  \|  \|  \|  \|  \|  \|  \|<br>Note:<br>delta SINR1 = estimated SINR by EE processing – ideal SINR 	[dB]<br>delta SINR2 = estimated SINR by regular processing – ideal SINR 	[dB]<br>delta SINR3 = estimated SINR by EE processing - estimated SINR by regular processing  [dB] | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| Agreement | For studying DL WUS monitoring during active time, the following schemes are considered for performance comparison, e.g., UE energy efficiency gain, UPT, resource overhead, network complexity:<br>- Baseline: PDCCH monitoring according to C-DRX cycle<br>- Scheme 0: DL WUS monitoring during active time<br>- Scheme 1: PDCCH skipping during active time<br>- Scheme 2: SSSG switching during active time<br>- Scheme 3: PDCCH monitoring according to shorter C-DRX cycle<br>- Scheme 4: Early termination of active time by network indication<br>- Scheme 5: DLWUS monitoring +SSSG switching during active time<br>- For all schemes, DL WUS monitoring outside active time is assumed.<br>- Note: In addition to C-DRX configuration agreed in 6GR power consumption model, companies can report other C-DRX configuration. Companies use the same C-DRX configuration for scheme 0, scheme1, scheme2, scheme 4, and scheme 5.<br>- Companies to report the UE behavior after receiving DL-WUS during active time.<br>- Note 1: companies are encouraged to provide results for scheme 0 vs scheme 1, scheme 0 vs scheme 2, scheme 0 vs scheme 3, scheme 0 vs scheme 4, scheme 0 vs scheme 5<br>- Note 2: companies to report the assumptions of SSSG monitoring periodicity when applicable.<br>- Note 3: the study here focus on the performance of DL WUS monitoring during active time | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| Agreement | For DL WUS operation in RRC_CONNECTED state, the DL WUS MOs (if more than one) monitored by a UE are TDMed, and the UE is not required to monitor more than one MO at given time. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |

## 10.6.2 Uplink WUS and operation

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.6 WUS and operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | - At least for UL WUS use cases (if supported) for UEs in idle/inactive mode, 6GR PRACH defined in AI 10.5.1.2 will be selected for UL WUS,<br>  - Further study whether enhancements are required beyond 6GR PRACH, e.g., considering<br>    - Robustness with respect to UL synchronization error<br>    - Detection performance including false alarm rate, missed detection rate, false detection rate, auto/cross correlation properties, etc.<br>    - Coverage aspect if aNB receives UL WUS with reduced TRxRUs<br>    - UL WUS sequence that can be shared by UEs in the cell/carrier<br>    - Detection complexity for BS<br>    - Implementation complexity for UE<br>    - Cubic metric/PAPR<br>    - Number of UL WUS sequences per UL WUS occasion<br>    - Shared PRACH and UL WUS occasion (impact to PRACH if shared) or separate PRACH and UL WUS occasions.<br>    - Inter-cell interference<br>    - UL WUS resource overhead<br>    - NW/UE energy consumption | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |

| Agreement | Modify the following previous agreement as follows in red:<br>For S band GEO:<br>- Set 6G-1: Reuse GEO Set 1 from 38.821<br>- Set 6G-2: Reuse GEO Set 1 from 38.821 with the following modifications:<br>  - 30m antenna diameter instead of 22m antenna diameter.<br>  - Antenna gain of 55dBi instead of 51dBi<br>  - G/T of 23dB/K instead of 19dB/K.<br>  - NOTE: EIRP density remains the same<br>  - EIRP density of 63 dBW/MHz instead of 59 dBW/MHz | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610529](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610529.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
| Agreement | For the 6GR NTN, RAN1 to confirm the 12 cm Ka/Ku-band terminal for small-terminal evaluation and agree on a consistent set of antenna and RF assumptions for determining its G/T and EIRP.<br>- As part of study, RAN1 to study aspect of this terminal including off-axis emissions (e.g. by companies providing the corresponding antenna radiation pattern).<br>- FFS: Exact G/T and peak EIRP values, considering the currently proposed parameter sets:<br>  - Ka-band: G/T in the range −4 to +2 dB/K and peak EIRP in the range of  61–67 dBm.<br>  - Ku-band: G/T in the range of  −9 to −6 dB/K and peak EIRP in the range of 51–53 dBm. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610529](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610529.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |

Each cell (which may comprise multiple hexagons) may have an independent scheduling entity. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610530](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610530.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
| Agreement | For the 6GR NTN, 12 cm Ka/Ku-band terminal:<br>- For Ka band:<br>  - Max G/T =−2.8dB/K<br>  - Max EIRP = 61.2dBm<br>- For Ku band:<br>  - Max G/T =−7.8 dB/K<br>  - Max EIRP = 51.2 dBm | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610530](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610530.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
| Agreement | For beam hopping in 6GR NTN, RAN1 to study the following aspects:<br>- Beam-specific or cell-specific DTX/DRX to match beam hopping<br>- Adaptation of procedures and scheduling of common channels (e.g. SIB, paging, RACH, RAR) and dedicated channels to match beam hopping.<br>- Dynamic beam activation/deactivation to adapt beam hopping to traffic requirements.<br>- Interaction of above with wide and narrow beams in hierarchical beam structure | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610530](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610530.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
| Agreement | In 6GR NTN, under the label of “physical layer aspects of multi-satellite operation”, at least the following sub-aspects are studied in “For physical layer aspects related to mobility”:<br>- Identify gaps (if any) in the NR design for the following:<br>  - RACH-less handover<br>  - Satellite switch, including:<br>    - Soft and hard satellite switch with resynchronization<br>    - With or without same PCI.<br>    - Fast satellite switching/recovery upon blockage, including applicability of BFR.<br>NOTE: For the aspect above it is assumed that the UE is only connected for data transmission to one satellite at a time. | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610531](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610531.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |

(More factors to be added if identified) ... \|<br>Note: The above is just an example. | Agreement | - For profile-based measurement,<br>  - Window(s) of consecutive samples applies in one profile including one or any combination of {delay, Doppler, angle} dimensions can be used<br>    - Candidate dimensions/combinations of dimensions are {delay, Doppler, angle, delay-Doppler, Doppler-angle, delay-angle, delay-Doppler-angle}<br>    - FFS: multiple windows of consecutive samples in profile<br>  - Note: the necessity, applicable sensing modes/entity, reporting aspects and achievable sensing performance can be discussed separately | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2609387](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2609387.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |

For scattering point/object-based metrics<br>    - Information to sensing receiver<br>      - Information related to location and/or velocity of the scattering point/object<br>      - If location/velocity of TRP/UE as sensing transmitter is used to derive the metrics, location/velocity of TRP/UE as sensing transmitter<br>- Note: the availability of TRP/UE locations depend on further discussion on privacy concern in relevant WGs<br>- Note: For bistatic sensing mode, privacy concern on sharing TRP/UE location to other TRPs/UEs is up to relevant WGs, e.g., SA3<br>- Note: the discussion on assistance information doesn’t imply a metric Level or sensing mode is to be specified for 6G ISAC<br>- Note: The inclusion of an assistance information in the above list does not imply that it is required for every sensing metric/reporting method, sensing mode or use case.<br>- Note: the applicable sensing modes/entity should be discussed for each of above candidate information<br>- Note: information for sensing processing can be discussed separately<br>- Note: it is not implied that the listed assistance information to sensing receiver need to be transmitted between different sensing entities<br>- Note: it is not implied that the listed assistance information to sensing receiver will be specified in WI work | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2609387](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2609387.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |

| Agreement | - The following candidates of reference path/sample for sensing measurement are identified in the study of 6G ISAC<br>  - Reference paths<br>    - The reference path may be the first detected path between STX and SRX<br>    - The reference path may be a non-first detected path between STX and SRX<br>    - The reference path may be the strongest path between STX and SRX<br>  - Reference samples<br>    - The reference sample may be the first sample in a window of consecutive samples<br>    - The reference sample may be a non-first sample in a window of consecutive samples<br>    - The reference sample may be the strongest sample in a window of consecutive samples<br>- FFS applicability to different sensing metrics<br>- FFS multiple reference paths/samples<br>- Note: It doesn't imply one or multiple candidates of reference path/sample will be introduced in normative phase | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610979](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610979.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - For profile-based measurement, the following solutions for further reporting overhead reduction for profile-based measurement can be considered including the necessity of further reporting overhead reduction for 6G ISAC study,<br>  - Reporting top N strongest samples in the window(s)<br>  - Reporting samples with power above threshold<br>  - Differential reporting of samples across time<br>  - Quantization schemes<br>  - Event-triggered reporting<br>  - Filtering/extracting reporting samples in one or more windows in profile<br>  - Combination of above solutions<br>  - Other solutions are not precluded<br>- Note: the applicable sensing modes/entity, and achievable sensing performance can be discussed separately<br>- Note: It doesn't imply one or multiple candidates of solutions will be introduced in normative phase | [Chair notes RAN1#127_eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23127_eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2610979](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_127/Docs/R1-2610979.zip)<br>`[127-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
